Built new test_analytics_fires.js (real Chromium load of a generated `/tokens/<slug>` page, intercepts `Analytics.track`) — it failed against the unmodified generator: zero events, a genuine wiring bug, not a traffic gap. Root cause: `renderAnalyticsBootstrap()`'s guard `if (window.Analytics && ...)` was always false because `analytics.js` declares `const Analytics = {...}` at the top of a classic (non-module) script — top-level `const` never becomes a `window` property, only a same-scope bare identifier visible to later classic `<script>` tags (confirmed empirically by both builder and verifier with a minimal 2-script-tag fixture in the repo's own Chromium). `app.js` calls `Analytics.trackPageView` unguarded (bare identifier), which is why the app's own page views tracked fine — the bug only lived in the generator-injected bootstrap's defensive check, silently zeroing page_view on all ~2,133 generated `/tokens/*`+`/chains/*` pages regardless of traffic. Fixed the one-line guard in generate-token-pages.js and generate-stories.js (`typeof Analytics !== 'undefined'`); generate-chain-pages.js imports the same function so chain pages fixed transitively. `pool_view(source=url_direct)` was already live-render-verified sound by an existing test_search.js check from 020 — no fix needed there, reran standalone and still green (20/20). Verifier independently reproduced the fail→pass transition via git show against origin/main's pre-fix generator, reran all touched/adjacent test suites (test_token_pages 59/59, test_chain_pages 53/53, test_stories 15/15, test_search 20/20), and confirmed test_smoke.js's failure is pre-existing (unpkg.com egress blocked in-sandbox, identical on unmodified main via git worktree). Added two new Cache-Control header rules to vercel.json: /fonts/(.*) gets `public, max-age=31536000, immutable` (one font file, never content-modified in repo history — safe); /(.*)\.(js|css) gets `public, max-age=300, stale-while-revalidate=86400` instead of the naive immutable recipe — filenames (app.compiled.min.js, style.min.css, etc.) are unhashed and this loop merges to main (auto-deploying via Vercel) multiple times a day, so a 1-year immutable cache would risk serving stale/broken JS to repeat visitors for up to a year after any deploy; 5min-fresh + 1day-SWR gives most of the perf win (kills same-session re-fetch) while a deploy still reaches a returning visitor on their next request after one background refetch. `/` and `/home` (the sacred __APP_MODE router entry points) left untouched at max-age=0, must-revalidate. New test_cache_headers.js (7 assertions, offline, parses vercel.json directly) wired into npm test; package.json's test-script chain is the only other file touched (no new dependencies). Verifier independently ran test_cache_headers.js (7/7), diffed vercel.json/home.html/plan.html/rewrites against origin/main to confirm zero drift outside the two new header rules, and ran the fast node-test suite itself rather than trusting reported output; test_smoke.js's render-path Playwright assertions unrunnable in-sandbox (yields.llama.fi/unpkg.com network-blocked), same documented precedent as 040/044/045/051-054 — this diff touches no render path (headers only), flagged needs human/network spot-check on next live deploy anyway per the render-path-merge convention. specs/055-pr.md HIGH-tier explainer + 5-question quiz written before merge. Top-scored READY item (9.3, a bug at the `waitlist_submitted` funnel top — outranks any experiment; SUBSCRIPTION is "the product's front door"). No prior spec existed → wrote specs/099.md from the BACKLOG row + a code-confirmed root-cause trace. ROOT CAUSE: the subscription pick handler skips the amount step and seeds `capital = Math.ceil(foreverNumber(target, guidanceApy)/100)*100` at the conservative guidance rate (`guidanceApy = stableGuidanceApy || 5.5`, planner.js:3592/3863) ≈ $4,400-4,800; the bloom's headline (`subHeroWinCovers`) and forever/mix-total line (`mixTotal`) recompute coverage from `currentMixStats.neededCapital` at the LIVE blended apy (10.58% → ≈$2,300), but the checkout hero card reads `slideCapital` (via `chkBaseCapital`, planner.js:2628) which was still the stale seed; the `slideCapital`⇄`neededCapital` sync effect (planner.js:1529) had a `!mixTouched` early-return so it only realigned AFTER the user touched the mix — the untouched default state diverged (same class as 085's coveredBundle-default: a value seeded at one rate leaking onto a surface recomputed at another). FIX (single planner.js hunk): dropped the `!mixTouched` gate + `mixTouched` dep from the sync effect so `slideCapital` tracks the live-apy `neededCapital` from the initial seed on — one source of truth across the hero card, headline and forever line (and now refreshes on persona/APY change too, previously stale until the mix was touched). Chose the STATE-level sync over a `chkBaseCapital`-only patch deliberately: the plan strip (planner.js:2758) + make-it-yours summary (2828) also read `slideCapital`, so a hero-only patch would have left THOSE on the stale seed — syncing the state fixes every consumer at once and keeps the capital slider functional. Safe by construction: the amount step is skipped for subs so `capital` is always a seeded default (no user-typed value to clobber), and the effect is keyed on `neededCapital` not `slideCapital` so dragging the capital slider (an explicit override) never refires it. No user-facing string added/changed → no translations.js edit needed (EN+KO already correct). BUG: parseNaturalLanguageQuery's SECOND token-parse fallback (app.js ~L277-297, the `if (!token && tokenCandidateText)` block) uses a bare `tokenCandidateText.includes(tokenLower)` substring test whose `chainNames` guard listed only the 8 LONG chain names, so 3+char short aliases that are substrings of a long chain name (`eth`⊂ethereum, `sol`⊂solana, `arb`⊂arbitrum) leaked into the token slot — a chain-only search got silently narrowed to a token (quiet trust erosion on the analytics search, the SEO arrival surface feeding the card funnel; op/2-char aliases don't leak, below the t.length>=3 gate; bnb/avax/etc caught by the exact-word wordsAfterChainIndicators skip). FIX (single app.js hunk): expanded that ONE `chainNames` array from 8 long names to all 24 `chainAliases` keys (short+long), plus a comment marking the sync requirement (chainAliases is in TDZ there, can't be referenced). Safe because the fallback is only reached when the primary WORD-BOUNDARY loop already found no token, so any substring-of-a-chain-word pick is definitionally the bug — a legit whole-word token (usdc on ethereum→USDC) is caught by the primary loop and never reaches the fallback (proven by regression asserts). FIX: lazy `resolvePlaywright()` (bare require → `npm root -g` → hardcoded global fallback, first hit wins, resolved source+version logged and recorded in the artifact because the global 1.56.1 is a DEGRADED path against the declared ^1.61.1 — territory note 2); resolution runs as `runAudit()`'s first action so a missing engine starts no server; success gains `status:'OK'` + `playwright{source,version}`; ANY fatal error now writes `{status:'DID_NOT_RUN', reason, attempts, surfacesCovered:[], findings:[]}` to the REAL out path — overwriting the stale verdict is the point, not a hazard — and exits non-zero; exit codes made explicit (0 clean / 1 P0-P1 / 2 fatal / 3 unresolvable) with item 142's P0/P1 gate lifted verbatim into an exported `blockingFindings()` (sole addition: an `f &&` null-guard) so that contract is testable without a full audit run. Two documented env hooks: `AUDIT_PLAYWRIGHT_ROOT` (collapses lookup to one root — the only way to make "unresolvable" reproducible on a machine that HAS node_modules) and `AUDIT_OUT` (so tests never clobber the real signals file). New `test_audit_runner.js`, 9 assertions, ~0.5s, no browser launch, wired into `npm test`; the only package.json edit is that one chain entry, zero dependency change. Real unassisted `node audit-app.js` run: exit 0, resolved from global (1.56.1), status OK, all 10 surfaces, 0 findings — matching the spec's own evidence, and the regenerated `signals/audit-findings.json` was KEPT (first artifact carrying the new fields; verifier judged writes into `signals/` to be the tool's normal output, not a drive-by). The behavioural change is TWO LINES; everything else is the constants, one formatter, tests, and regenerated output. THE RAILS THEMSELVES ARE BYTE-UNTOUCHED — this only APPLIES an existing rail to a surface that never had one, which is why the NEVER list (which forbids *weakening* a rail) points the other way. LIVE RESULT: top line goes `zeebu · ZBU — 353114.2% APY, $576,877 TVL` → `uniswap-v3 · WETH-USDC — 47.2% APY, $112,787,401 TVL`; lines >1000% go 8 → 0 (`llms.txt`) and 15 → 0 (`llms-full.txt`), with 8 and 15 real pool lines still standing (spec floor ≥5, so the fix did not empty the surface — a distinct failure wearing the same "no violations" badge, per the playbook trap). After `npm ci` (67 packages, 3s) the same subset was 34 pass / 2 fail and both remaining reds were a 90s cap firing on files that transitively drive Chromium — the reds were environmental, which is item 149's finding generalised from one file to the ship gate itself. FIX: `run-tests.js` parses `test:serial` (the original 94-step chain, preserved byte-identical, plus the one new test file appended) as the SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH for the file list, so a file cannot be dropped from the gate by editing one place; preflights node_modules with one actionable line and exit 2, running zero files; classifies plain vs browser by a memoised, cycle-safe, depth-limited TRANSITIVE scan of local require edges — the operator's own first probe used a one-level grep and misclassified test_seo_surface_audit.js, test_audit_prescan.js and test_audit_app.js, which name no browser driver but require ./audit-app.js, which does; runs each file in its own child process with a per-file timeout and bounded concurrency, browser lane forced serial because audit-app.js binds fixed port 8821 and 162-notes.md records a live EADDRINUSE collision; NEVER aborts on a red; reports per-file PASS/FAIL/TIMEOUT + duration + summary and exits non-zero iff anything failed or timed out. RESULT: plain lane 34 pass / 0 fail / 0 timeout in ~7s — the first fully-observed lane result the loop has ever recorded — with no product red revealed. POST-VERIFICATION CHANGE, made after the PASS and separately re-verified: the verifier flagged that a flat 120s default would record TIMEOUT for legitimately-slow browser files (test_search.js needs ~550s by 158's own measurement, test_smoke.js exceeded 120s), so a default `npm test` could never exit 0 — the default is now lane-aware, 120s plain / 600s browser, with an explicit --timeout= still overriding both; shipping the version that PASSED would have replaced an unobservable gate with a permanently-red one, which is arguably worse because an always-red gate gets ignored. TWO DEVIATIONS, both documented and both consequences of a program that reads its own repository: a `require.main === module` guard (main() was unconditional, so the test file requiring the module kicked off a real test-chain run as a side effect — caught live when stray test_smoke.js children started spawning), and a self-reference workaround because the classifier substring-scans source for the driver's package name, which run-tests.js's own source necessarily contains, so a literal require path or even the name in a comment made the test file classify ITSELF as browser. HONESTLY NOT CLAIMED: the full 61-file browser lane exceeds the 5-minute foreground timebox by construction and was NOT run — 3 files were driven end-to-end through the runner across builder and verifier (test_landing.js PASS 40.6s in the verifier's own run), 58 were not; spec A7 made the bounded claim the deliverable and a full-chain-green claim an automatic FAIL.
